  For equal-mass real weights $p,q$ and $0\le f\le1$,
  $|\sum_xp(x)f(x)-\sum_xq(x)f(x)|\le d(p,q)$.
  On nonempty seed and output alphabets, the weighted strong-extractor
  test contract is equivalent to distance at most $\varepsilon$ from the
  independent uniform seed-output law for every normalized capped source.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The positive and negative parts of $p-q$ have equal total mass, each
  equal to its total variation. A statistic in $[0,1]$ is bounded by
  these parts. The extractor equivalence uses the equal-mass finite-test
  characterization of total variation.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Strong extraction with an average leakage budget]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-average-leakage}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.leakage_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.leakage_average_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-distance,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-bounded-expectations}
  Let $E$ be a weighted strong extractor with threshold $K$ and
  nonnegative error $\varepsilon$, on nonempty seed and output alphabets.
  If $p(t,x)$ is a probability law and $0\le p(t,x)\le\mu(t)$, extraction
  from $x$ with an independent uniform seed has distance at most
  $\varepsilon+K\sum_t\mu(t)$ from uniform, retaining both the entire
  tag $t$ and the seed. No cap on every conditional row is assumed.
  This is the finite average-entropy step in Chattopadhyay--Liao,
  Lemma~3.26, which credits Chattopadhyay--Goodman--Liao and ideas from
  Chattopadhyay--Li (2016).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  A positive row with $K\mu(t)\le p_T(t)$ satisfies the extractor's
  conditional cap. Other rows have total mass at most
  $K\sum_t\mu(t)$ and contribute distance at most their mass. Null
  rows contribute zero. Sum the row distances and average over the seed.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Factored sources and two-sided extraction laws]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-laws}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.factoredWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedSeedWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.leakageSourceWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.twoSidedExtractionWeight}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-weights}
  A shared transcript has law $w(z)$, and the left and right variables
  have conditionally independent laws $l_z(a),r_z(b)$. The source
  $x(z,a)$ and observation $u(z,a)$ depend on the left side; the seed
  $y(z,b)$ and observation $v(z,b)$ depend on the right. A further
  finite leak $L(z,v,a)$ may depend on the left variable and the right
  observation together. The actual output law retains
  $(z,b,u(z,a),L(z,v(z,b),a))$ alongside $E(x(z,a),y(z,b))$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Extraction with two-sided finite leakage]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-leakage}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.twoSidedExtractionWeight_eq_map,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.twoSidedExtractionWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.two_sided_leakage_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.two_sided_leakage_dist_le_of_budget}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-laws,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-average-leakage,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-bounded-expectations}
  Let $w,l_z,r_z$ be probability laws and $E$ a threshold-$K$ strong
  extractor of nonnegative error $\varepsilon$, with nonempty seed and
  output alphabets. Suppose a nonnegative envelope $\mu(z,u)$ bounds
  $\Pr[Z=z,U=u,X=x]$ for every $x$, and the law of $(Z,V,Y)$ is within
  $\delta$ of its actual $(Z,V)$ marginal with an independent uniform
  seed. The actual retained extraction law has distance at most
  \[
    \varepsilon+\delta+K|\mathcal L|\sum_{z,u}\mu(z,u)
  \]
  from its actual retained marginal with an independent uniform output,
  where $\mathcal L$ is the extra leak's alphabet. A bound
  $K|\mathcal L|\sum\mu\le\rho$ gives error $\varepsilon+\delta+\rho$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  For the independent uniform seed, apply the average leakage theorem
  with tag $(z,v,u,L)$. Refining an event reduces its point mass;
  averaging over $v$ leaves envelope total $|\mathcal L|\sum\mu$.
  Conditional independence expresses both actual and uniform-seed
  distances as expectations of the same $[0,1]$-valued row statistic.
  The bounded-expectation inequality therefore charges the seed
  discrepancy once, while retaining all of the right variable.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The actual independence-merging output law]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-independence-merging}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.independenceMergingWeight}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-laws}
  Given transcript $Z$, let the entire left tuple
  $(X,X_1,\ldots,X_t)$ be independent of the entire right tuple
  $(Y,Y_1,\ldots,Y_t)$. For index sets $S,T\subseteq[t]$, the actual
  law applies $E$ to each source-seed pair and retains
  $(Z,Y,Y_1,\ldots,Y_t,(E(X_i,Y_i))_{i\in S\cup T})$ alongside
  $E(X,Y)$. It is the deterministic image of the factored source law,
  and $S,T$ may overlap.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Finite independence merging with the average entropy budget]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-independence-merging}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.independence_merging_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.independence_merging_dist_le_of_budget}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-independence-merging,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-leakage,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-distance}
  Let the transcript and both conditional tuple laws be normalized, and
  let $E$ be a threshold-$K$ strong extractor of nonnegative error
  $\varepsilon$, with nonempty seed and output alphabets. Suppose
  $\mu(z,u)\ge0$ bounds every joint mass
  $\Pr[Z=z,X_T=u,X=x]$, and $(Z,Y_S,Y)$ is within $\delta$ of its
  actual $(Z,Y_S)$ marginal with an independent uniform seed.
  The independence-merging law is within
  \[
    \varepsilon+\delta+K|\mathrm{Out}|^{|S|}\sum_{z,u}\mu(z,u)
  \]
  of its actual retained marginal with a fresh independent uniform
  output. If the last term is at most $\varepsilon$, the error is
  $2\varepsilon+\delta$. This is the finite envelope form of
  Chattopadhyay--Liao, Lemma~3.26; the envelope total expresses the
  average conditional guessing budget without logarithms.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply two-sided leakage with $U=X_T$, $V=Y_S$, and the extra leak
  $(E(X_i,Y_i))_{i\in S}$, whose alphabet has size
  $|\mathrm{Out}|^{|S|}$. The retained source copies $X_T$ and all
  right-side seeds recover every remaining output indexed by $T$.
  On overlaps use the already retained $S$ output. Processing side
  information preserves the actual uniform comparison and contracts
  distance. The numerical budget gives the stated final error.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Transcript updates after deterministic messages]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-observed-transcript}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscriptWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscriptKernel}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-weights,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-laws}
  Given transcript law $w(z)$, source kernel $p_z(a)$, and deterministic
  message $f(z,a)$, the new transcript law is
  $w'(z,u)=w(z)\Pr[f(z,A)=u\mid z]$. Its updated source kernel is the
  conditional law of $A$ on that message, completed uniformly on null
  rows. Both original source coordinates remain available after updating.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Exact factorization after adaptive observations]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-observed-transcript}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.factoredWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscriptWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscriptKernel_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscriptWeight_mul_kernel,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.factoredWeight_observe_left,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.factoredWeight_observe_right,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.factoredWeight_observe_left_right,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscript_left_right_probability}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-observed-transcript,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-distance}
  A left-side observation updates its conditional kernel while preserving
  the right kernel; a right-side observation has the symmetric property.
  The resulting factored law is exactly the pushforward of the original
  law retaining the message and both original side variables. Every
  updated kernel and transcript is normalized. A left message followed
  by a right message depending on that first message has the same exact
  factorization. These rules include null observation rows and formalize
  the deterministic-conditioning step of Chattopadhyay--Liao, Lemma~3.25.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Multiplying the updated kernel by its transcript weight recovers the
  original joint mass on the observed event and is zero elsewhere.
  Finite pushforward sums then identify the complete factored law.
  Compose the two exact pushforwards for the adaptive observation.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Marginal replacement preserving an external transcript]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-coupling}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_conditional_marginal_replacement_kernel,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_conditional_joint_marginal_replacement}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-coupling,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-distance}
  Let $p$ be a probability law of $(Z,A,B)$ and let $q$ be a probability
  law of $(Z,A')$ with the same $Z$ marginal. There is a coupling
  preserving the complete original $(Z,A,B)$ law, giving $(Z,A')$ law
  $q$, and satisfying
  $\Pr[A\ne A']=d(p_{ZA},q)$. Its kernel makes $A'$ independent of
  $B$ given $(Z,A)$, including null rows. The projected law $p'$ of
  $(Z,A',B)$ preserves $(Z,B)$ jointly and satisfies
  $d(p,p')=d(p_{ZA},q)$. No per-transcript error bound is required.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Normalize each transcript row, lift a maximal marginal coupling
  through its original conditional kernel, and multiply by the common
  transcript mass. Summing exact row disagreements gives the global
  joint marginal distance. Projection gives the upper bound on joint
  distance; marginal contraction gives the matching lower bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Tag-dependent output bijections preserve uniformity distance]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-output-bijections}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.mapWeight_uniformSecond_fiberEquiv,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.weightDist_uniformSecond_fiberEquiv,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.mapWeight_uniformSecond_add_right,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.weightDist_uniformSecond_add_right}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-distance}
  For arbitrary finite real weights $p(a,b)$ and output equivalences
  $e_a:B\simeq C$, the map $(a,b)\mapsto(a,e_a(b))$ commutes with
  uniformizing the output and preserves the exact distance $d(p,U_2p)$.
  Adding a tag-dependent mask in a finite additive group is a special case.
  The statements require neither normalization nor nonnegative weights.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Each fiber bijection preserves its cardinality and uniform law, while
  the first marginal is unchanged. Apply deterministic contraction in
  both directions of the complete tagged equivalence.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Retained affine extraction laws]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-initialization}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainedSeedWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainedExtractionWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineExtractionWeight}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-laws}
  Sample $(Z,A,B)$ from a factored law. A left source $x(Z,A)$ and
  right seed $y(Z,B)$ give the retained law of
  $(Z,B,E(x(Z,A),y(Z,B)))$. The affine version first combines the
  left source with a right mask $m(Z,B)$ using a specified operation,
  and applies the same extractor and actual seed. Both are deterministic
  images of the original factored law and retain the whole right variable.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Affine initial extraction with complete right information]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-initialization}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainedExtractionWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineExtractionWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineExtractionWeight_eq_map,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.retained_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.affine_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-initialization,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-leakage,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-output-bijections}
  Let the factor laws be normalized and $E$ be a threshold-$K$ strong
  extractor of nonnegative error $\varepsilon$, with nonempty seed and
  output alphabets. Suppose $\mu(z)\ge0$ bounds every joint source mass
  $\Pr[Z=z,x(Z,A)=x]$, and $(Z,y(Z,B))$ is within $\delta$ of its
  actual transcript law with a fresh uniform seed. Retained extraction
  has distance at most $\varepsilon+\delta+K\sum_z\mu(z)$ from its
  actual $(Z,B)$ marginal with independent uniform output. The same bound
  holds on the combined input whenever each fixed mask and seed acts
  bijectively on the extractor output. The mask may be correlated
  arbitrarily with the seed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Specialize two-sided leakage to singleton observation and leakage
  alphabets, retaining all of $B$. For the masked input, the extractor's
  equivariance identifies its actual law with a tagged output bijection
  of the unmasked law. Exact distance preservation adds no error.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The actual scheduled program on a Boolean affine source]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-affine-initialization}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockBooleanExtractor_affine_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockBooleanExtractor_dyadic_affine_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-initialization,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-boolean}
  The actual scheduled Boolean extractor, under its proved finite
  budget and reserve conditions, has the affine retained-law guarantee
  with error $\varepsilon_{\rm schedule}+\delta+K\sum\mu$ on a left
  source XOR a right mask. The explicit dyadic schedule gives
  $\varepsilon_{\rm schedule}=2^{-e}$. These are the same bit programs
  and exact Boolean seed widths already supplied by the uniform runtime
  construction. No extractor or finite-field instance is a caller premise.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Use the proved weighted extraction guarantee and fixed-seed XOR law
  of the actual program. Coordinatewise XOR with the mask output is
  an involution on the complete block-output tuple. Apply affine initial
  extraction, preserving the actual full right-side marginal.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Head and tail structure of block sources]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-conditioning}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.IsBlockSource.exists_head_tail,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.isBlockSource_prepend,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.isBlockSource_of_prepend_mixture}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-source-structure}
  Every threshold-$K$ source on $t+1$ blocks admits a normalized capped
  head law $w$ and normalized threshold-$K$ tail laws $p_a$ such that
  $p(a,z)=w(a)p_a(z)$ for all heads and tails. Conversely these conditions
  give a block source. More generally, nonnegative coefficients $c(h,a)$
  with row totals equal to a normalized capped head law give a block
  source by mixing the tail laws within each head row.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Normalize each positive head row. Assign a fixed positive row's
  conditional law to zero-mass heads; normalization guarantees such a row
  exists. Prefix-mass identities transfer every conditional cap. For the
  converse, sum the tail inequalities with the nonnegative row coefficients.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Correlated head repair with block-source tails]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-tail-repair}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_block_tail_replacement}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-coupling,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-conditioning,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-tagged-distance}
  Sample a latent value $a$ from a probability law $w$, and a conditional
  threshold-$K$ block-source tail $Z_a$. Given a deterministic head $f(a)$
  and any normalized threshold-$K$ head law $v$, there is a threshold-$K$
  block source $R$ with head marginal $v$ such that
  \[
    d\bigl((f(a),Z_a),R\bigr)=d(f(a),v).
  \]
  No independence between the latent value and its tail is required.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Replace the first marginal of the joint law of $(f(a),a)$, preserving
  the latent marginal and paying exactly the head distance. Attach the
  same normalized conditional tail to each latent value. Keeping all tags
  preserves the distance exactly; projection can only decrease it.
  The unchanged head distance gives the reverse inequality. The row-mixture
  criterion proves that the repaired tuple is a block source.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Successive pair splitting]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-split}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.splitBlockIndexEquiv,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.splitBlockEquiv}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-source}
  Split a tuple of $t$ pairs into $2t$ coordinates, preserving the order
  first coordinate, second coordinate, then the next pair. With zero-based
  indexing, pair $i$ occupies positions $2i$ and $2i+1$. This map is an
  equivalence and introduces no randomness.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Splitting every block into consecutive halves]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-split}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.IsBlockSource.exists_split_pow_two,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.splitBlockEquiv_apply_fst,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.splitBlockEquiv_apply_snd}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-split,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-block-repair,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-conditioning,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-tail-repair,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-tagged-distance}
  Let $p$ be a threshold-$2^k$ block source on $t$ pairs of values in an
  alphabet of size $2^m$. If $s\le m$ and $m+s+e\le k$, then its split
  law is within distance $t2^{-e}$ of a threshold-$2^s$ source on $2t$
  consecutive blocks. The original pairs can be dependent, with the input
  entropy bound conditioned on all earlier pairs. This is the finite
  power-of-two form of Chattopadhyay--Goodman--Liao, Corollary 5.4.
  The zero-block case is included without an upper bound on $k$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Disintegrate the original head pair and its block-source tails. Repair
  the pair into a two-block source while retaining its original joint cap.
  Correlated head replacement therefore gives a nearby source that still
  has the original threshold on all pair blocks. Disintegrate it again,
  split each conditional tail by induction, and prepend the repaired head
  coordinates. Deterministic contraction charges at most $2^{-e}$ for the
  head repair, and normalized conditional averaging charges at most
  $(t-1)2^{-e}$ for the remaining pairs. The triangle inequality gives the
  stated error; the empty tuple case has distance zero.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Splitting with an earlier retained seed]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-split-family}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_split_pow_two_seedFamily}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-split,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-tagged-distance}
  Under the preceding entropy and alphabet-size conditions, suppose that
  every fixing of an earlier uniform seed gives a threshold-$2^k$ source
  on $t$ pairs. Splitting the pairs while retaining that seed gives a joint
  law within $t2^{-e}$ of a uniform-seed family of threshold-$2^s$ sources
  on $2t$ blocks. The source may depend on the earlier seed, and splitting
  introduces no fresh seed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Choose the preceding splitting witness separately in each seed fiber.
  The seed-preserving deterministic map acts fiberwise, and exact distance
  averaging gives the same bound on the joint distribution.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Shared-seed block condensation]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-condenser}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededCondenser.block}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-strong,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-conditioning,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-tail-repair,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-tagged-distance}
  Let $C$ be a weighted strong condenser from threshold $K_{\rm in}$ to
  $K_{\rm out}$ with error $\varepsilon$, and let $(X_1,\ldots,X_t)$ be a
  threshold-$K_{\rm in}$ block source. For one independent uniform seed $Y$,
  \[
    (Y,C(X_1,Y),\ldots,C(X_t,Y))
  \]
  is within distance $t\varepsilon$ of a uniform-seed family of normalized
  threshold-$K_{\rm out}$ block sources. The blocks can be dependent;
  the seed appears only once. This proves the finite shared-seed conclusion
  of Chattopadhyay--Goodman--Liao, Lemma 5.5.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Induct on the number of blocks, conditioning on the original head.
  Correct each conditional tail by the induction hypothesis; averaging
  and deterministic processing cost at most $t\varepsilon$. Apply the
  condenser to the head marginal, and repair the head separately at each
  seed while retaining its latent value. Exact distance averaging bounds
  this second change by $\varepsilon$. The triangle inequality gives
  $(t+1)\varepsilon$. The empty tuple case has distance zero.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Shared-seed block extraction]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-extractor}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.block}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-condenser,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-condenser,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-source-structure}
  Applying a weighted strong extractor of error $\varepsilon$ to all $t$
  blocks with the same uniform seed has discrepancy at most $t\varepsilon$
  from independent uniform seed and output tuple on every joint test.
  The input is a block source at the extractor's threshold. Seed and output
  alphabets are nonempty.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Regard the extractor as a condenser at full output cardinality. Its
  conditional block-source witnesses are exactly uniform on the whole
  output tuple by full-entropy uniformity. Use the distance-to-test bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The scheduled condenser with one seed for all blocks]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-block-condenser}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.decodedExplicitCondenser_block,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.decodedExplicitCondenser_block_ofFn}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-condenser,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-programs}
  For positive rate $u$, apply the actual decoded scheduled condenser to
  every block of an $n$-bit threshold-$2^k$ block source, using one field
  seed. Its retained-seed output is within $t2^{-e}$ of a seed-conditioned
  threshold-$2^k$ block source. Both Boolean-vector inputs and arbitrary
  injective encodings into words of length $n$ are covered. This is a
  statistical theorem about the actual component maps; a uniform evaluator
  for the full multilevel recursion remains a separate construction.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Instantiate shared-seed block condensation with the proved weighted
  guarantee of the scheduled runtime condenser.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[One pair of seeds for the one-shot leaves]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-one-shot-blocks}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.decodedOneShotExtractor_block,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.decodedOneShotExtractor_block_ofFn}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-extractor,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-programs}
  For a source of $t$ blocks at threshold $2^{\ell+2e}$, the actual one-shot
  map can reuse the same pair of independent field seeds on all blocks.
  The entire tuple of $t$ outputs of $\ell$ bits has joint test discrepancy
  at most $t2^{-e}$ from uniform, retaining the field-seed pair once.
  Both fixed-length Boolean-vector inputs and generic injective word
  encodings are supported, without extra capacity or positivity premises.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply shared-seed block extraction to the proved weighted one-shot
  guarantee. The finite field and output types supply the required
  nonempty instances.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Adding a fresh seed while retaining earlier randomness]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-retained-seed-step}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainSeedEquiv,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainedSeedStep}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-strong}
  Given a joint weighting on an earlier seed and a current state, sample
  one fresh independent uniform seed, apply the specified deterministic
  step, and retain the ordered pair of seeds with the output. The new seed
  is independent of the entire old joint law.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Transport and composition of retained-seed steps]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-retained-seed-step}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.seedFamilyWeight_product,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.weightDist_seedFamilyWeight_product,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainedSeedStep_seedFamilyWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainedSeedStep_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.IsProbabilityWeight.retainedSeedStep,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainedSeedStep_weightedSeededOutput}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-retained-seed-step,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-tagged-distance}
  Retained-seed steps contract total variation and preserve probability
  when the fresh seed type is nonempty. On an exact earlier-seed family,
  the resulting conditional law is the deterministic image in each pair
  of seed values. Distances over a product seed average the fresh-seed
  joint distances over earlier seeds. Successive seeded programs equal
  their deterministic composition with both seeds retained.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Reassociate the seed coordinates by an equivalence and use product
  cardinality, exact tagged averaging, and deterministic contraction.
  Composition follows from composition of the finite pushforwards.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[One condense-and-split level with accumulated error]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-condense-split-level}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededCondenser.condenseSplit}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-retained-seed-step,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-condenser,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-split-family}
  Suppose the actual joint law of earlier seeds and $t$ current blocks
  is within $\eta$ of an earlier-seed family of threshold-$K$ block sources.
  Let a fresh-seed condenser preserve those blocks at output threshold
  $2^k$, with error $\varepsilon$ and pair-valued output over an alphabet
  of size $2^m$. If $s\le m$ and $m+s+e\le k$, condensing with one fresh
  seed shared by all blocks and then splitting gives a threshold-$2^s$
  block-source family at joint error at most
  $\eta+t(\varepsilon+2^{-e})$. Both earlier and fresh seeds are retained.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply block condensation separately at each earlier seed, averaging its
  joint fresh-seed error. Split the ideal pair-block family and contract
  the condenser comparison through the same split map. The preceding
  approximation contracts through the whole step. Two triangle inequalities
  add the three errors.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Final extraction with all earlier seeds retained]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-retained-block-extraction}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.retained_block,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.retained_block_tests}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-retained-seed-step,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-extractor}
  If the current joint law is $\eta$-close to a family of $t$ block sources
  at an extractor's threshold, a fresh-seed extractor of error
  $\varepsilon$, shared across the blocks, produces a joint law within
  $\eta+t\varepsilon$ of uniform on both seed coordinates and the entire
  output tuple. For normalized actual weights, the same bound holds on
  every joint test. The earlier and fresh seed types and output alphabet
  are nonempty.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Full-entropy block-condenser witnesses are uniform on all output tuples.
  Average their distance bounds over earlier seeds, and use contraction
  for the preceding joint error. Equal total mass gives the test bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Finite recursive block maps]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-map}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.RecursiveSeeds,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockCount,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockMap,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockError,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockExtractor}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-split,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-retained-seed-step}
  Starting from an initial seeded map, run $h$ levels, each using its own
  fresh seed to condense all current blocks and split their pair outputs.
  Block alphabets and seed types can vary with the level. Starting from
  $t$ blocks, level $i$ has $2^it$ blocks. The recursive extractor starts
  with a single condensed block and finally applies one shared-seed
  extractor to every leaf. Every seed is retained exactly once.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Finite recursive block-source guarantee]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-source}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_recursiveBlockSource,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockCount_eq}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-map,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-condense-split-level}
  Suppose the joint law of the initial seed and output is within $\eta$
  of a uniform-seed family of threshold-$2^{k_0}$ sources on $t$ blocks.
  At each executed level $i<h$, let the condenser
  preserve threshold $2^{k_i}$ with error $\varepsilon_i$, let its half-output
  alphabet have size $2^{m_i}$, and suppose
  \[
    k_{i+1}\le m_i,\qquad m_i+k_{i+1}+e_i\le k_i.
  \]
  The actual recursive map is within
  \[
    \eta+\sum_{i<h}2^it(\varepsilon_i+2^{-e_i})
  \]
  of a retained-seed family of threshold-$2^{k_h}$ sources on $2^ht$ blocks.
  No bound on each individual seed's error is assumed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Induct on the number of levels, applying the preceding level theorem to
  the actual recursive output. Exact seeded composition identifies its
  output with the next recursive map. The error recurrence is the displayed
  finite sum. Only hypotheses for the executed levels are used.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact seed cardinalities and finite error budget]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-budget}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveSeeds_card,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveSeeds_card_pow_two,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockError_const,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockError_dyadic_total,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockError_dyadic_budget}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-map}
  The retained seed alphabet has cardinality equal to the initial seed
  cardinality times the product of the $h$ fresh-seed cardinalities.
  Hence power-of-two seed widths add. With one initial block and all
  initial, local, splitting, and final extraction errors budgeted by
  $2^{-E}$, the total bound is
  $(3\cdot2^h-1)2^{-E}$. Choosing $E=e+h+2$ makes this at most $2^{-e}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Induct using product cardinality for the seed formula. Sum the doubled
  block counts for the local errors, and add the initial and leaf errors.
  The inequality $3\cdot2^h-1\le4\cdot2^h$ gives the dyadic budget.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Strong extraction by the finite recursive composition]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-extractor}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededCondenser.singleton,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededCondenser.recursiveExtractor,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededCondenser.recursiveExtractor_dyadic}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-source,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-budget,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-retained-block-extraction}
  Combine an initial strong condenser, the lossless level condensers
  satisfying the preceding finite entropy inequalities, and a final strong
  extractor at threshold $2^{k_h}$. The actual recursive function is a
  weighted strong extractor at the initial input threshold, with error
  at most the sum of the initial bound, the level budgets, and $2^h$ times
  the final component bound. In particular, local error exponent $e+h+2$
  gives final error at most $2^{-e}$. These are finite guarantees for the
  supplied components; no asymptotic schedule or uniform runtime claim is
  included in this theorem.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Transport the initial condenser witness to a single-coordinate block
  source. Apply the recursive guarantee, then final retained-seed block
  extraction. Exact seeded composition identifies the tested function with
  the recursive extractor. The finite dyadic budget gives the specialization.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Fixed-seed additivity of recursive maps]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-additivity}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.splitBlockEquiv_add,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.condenseSplitMap_add,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockMap_add,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockExtractor_add}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-map}
  If the initial map and every executed condenser preserve addition for
  each fixed seed, so does the recursive block map. If the final component
  also preserves addition, the recursive extractor does too. Only addition
  structures and the stated component equalities are needed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Successive splitting is a coordinate rearrangement, so it preserves
  pointwise addition. Induct through the level maps and compose with the
  final component equality at each output coordinate.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[A constant-rate recursive parameter schedule]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-parameters}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockEntropy,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockWidth,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockSeedWidth,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockReserve}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-pair-condenser}
  For depth $h$, leaf reserve $Q$, and common error exponent $E$, set
  $k_i=4^{h-i}Q$. Starting from width $N$, recursively set $n_{i+1}$ to
  the actual half-output width of the rate-three condenser on $n_i$ bits
  at entropy $k_i$ and exponent $E$. Record its actual field seed width
  $b_i$. The explicit reserve is $Q=4096(L+E+1)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Finite width, entropy, and work bounds]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-parameters}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockEntropy_last,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockEntropy_succ,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockWidth_bounds,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlock_split_budget,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockSeedWidth_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlock_payload_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlock_count_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-parameters,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-pair-split-budget}
  Write $T$ for the condenser budget on width $N$, entropy $k_0$, and
  exponent $E$. If $k_0\le N$ and $3(24T)+6E\le2Q$, then every executed
  level satisfies $k_{i+1}\le n_{i+1}$ and
  $n_{i+1}+k_{i+1}+E\le k_i$. For $i\le h$, one has
  $k_i\le n_i\le N$, $b_i\le24T$, and $2^in_i\le2N$.
  Also $k_h=Q$ and $k_i=4k_{i+1}$ for $i<h$.
  When $Q>0$, the block count satisfies $2^i\le k_0$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Induct on the level, using monotonicity of the condenser budget and
  the rate-three splitting inequality. The geometric entropy decrease
  bounds the width and total payload; positivity of $Q$ bounds the count.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[An explicit reserve pays all rounded split budgets]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-reserve}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockReserve_pos,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockEntropy_zero_clog_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockInitialWidth_bounds,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockInitialBudget_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockReserve_budget}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-parameters,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-pair-condenser}
  Suppose $\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil\le L$. Set $Q=4096(L+E+1)$,
  $k_0=4^hQ$, and let $N$ be the full output width of the actual rate-one
  condenser on $n$ bits at entropy $k_0$. Then $Q>0$,
  $k_0\le N\le64k_0$, and its next condenser budget satisfies
  \[
    T\le E+4h+2\lceil\log_2(L+E+1)\rceil+64.
  \]
  If also $h\le L$, then $3(24T)+6E\le2Q$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Bound the logarithm of $4^hQ+1$ by
  $2h+13+\lceil\log_2(L+E+1)\rceil$. The checked rounded field and
  output-rate bounds give the width and budget estimates. The reserve's
  coefficient absorbs the remaining integer slack.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The fully specified scheduled block extractor]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-extractor}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockInitialWidth,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.ScheduledInitialSeed,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.ScheduledLevelSeed,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.ScheduledFinalSeed,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockInitial,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockStep,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockExtractor,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockSeedBits}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-map,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-parameters,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-one-shot-program}
  Start with the actual rate-one condenser, run the scheduled rate-three
  paired condensers for $h$ levels, and apply the actual one-shot extractor
  with output length $\ell$ to all leaves. Its seed consists of one initial
  field seed, one fresh field seed per level, and one final field-seed pair
  shared by every leaf. Let $d$ be the sum of their field bit widths.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Strong extraction by the specified schedule]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-extractor}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockExtractor_weighted,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockExtractor_dyadic}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-extractor,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-reserve,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-extractor,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-programs}
  If $\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil\le L$ and $h\le L$, set
  $E=e+h+2$ and $Q=4096(L+E+1)$. The specified map is a weighted strong
  extractor from $n$-bit sources of min-entropy at least $4^hQ$ to
  $2^hL$ bits, with error at most $2^{-e}$. More generally, the global
  reserve budget and $\ell+2E\le Q$ give error
  $(3\cdot2^h-1)2^{-E}$ for leaf output length $\ell$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Instantiate the finite recursive extraction theorem with the actual
  initial, internal, and leaf maps. The explicit reserve pays every
  splitting inequality and the leaf extractor's entropy requirement.
  The dyadic error budget gives the stated final error. This follows the
  recursive method of Chattopadhyay--Goodman--Liao,
  \href{https://eccc.weizmann.ac.il/report/2021/075/}{Theorem 5.6 of
  \emph{Affine Extractors for Almost Logarithmic Entropy}} (2021), with our
  coarser factor-four entropy schedule rather than their sharper bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact and bounded seed size of the specified schedule]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-seeds}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.card_scheduledBlockSeeds,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockSeedBits_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-extractor,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-budget,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-reserve,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-one-shot-parameters}
  The actual retained seed type has cardinality $2^d$ for all natural
  parameters. Under $\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil\le L$ and $h\le L$, with
  $Q=4096(L+E+1)$ and leaf output length $L$, its width satisfies
  \[
    d\le8192\bigl(L+(h+1)(E+h+
      \lceil\log_2(L+E+1)\rceil+1)\bigr).
  \]
  This is a finite bound on the specified seed type.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Multiply the proved field cardinalities along the actual seed tuple.
  Bound the initial seed by its input budget, every level seed by the
  common compressed budget, and the final shared pair by the one-shot
  estimates. Sum these bounds once per retained seed.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Fixed-seed XOR law of the specified extractor]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-linearity}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockExtractor_xor}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-extractor,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-additivity,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-one-shot-program}
  For every fixed complete seed tuple and all natural parameters, the
  scheduled extractor sends XOR of two Boolean inputs to coordinatewise
  addition of their outputs in $\mathbf F_2$. No entropy or error-budget
  premise is needed for this identity.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply recursive additivity to the actual condenser, its serialization
  into Boolean halves, and the actual one-shot leaf extractor.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The fixed polylogarithmic-output family]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-polylog-extractor}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.polylogBlockLength,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.polylogBlockDepth,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.polylogBlockErrorExponent,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.polylogBlockReserve,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.polylogBlockEntropy,

    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.polylogBlockExtractor}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-extractor}
  Fix natural parameters $a,e$. At input length $n$, take
  $L=\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil$, raw depth
  $h=a\lceil\log_2(L+1)\rceil$, $E=e+h+2$, and
  $Q=4096(L+E+1)$. Instantiate the specified extractor with leaf length
  $L$, source entropy threshold $k=4^hQ$, and its actual retained seeds.
  These family definitions do not truncate the depth at small lengths.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Logarithmic seed, sublinear entropy, and polylogarithmic output]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-polylog-extractor}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.eventually_polylogBlockDepth_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.polylogBlockEntropy_isLittleO,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.eventually_polylogBlockEntropy_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.eventually_polylogBlockSeedBits_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.card_polylogBlockSeeds,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.polylogBlockOutputBits_bounds,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.eventually_polylogBlockExtractor}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-polylog-extractor,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-extractor,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-seeds}
  For every fixed $a,e$, eventually $h\le L$ and the actual map is a
  weighted strong extractor at source support threshold $2^k$ and error
  $2^{-e}$. Its entropy threshold satisfies $k=o(n)$, so eventually $k\le n$.
  Its actual seed alphabet has cardinality $2^d$, with eventually
  $d\le16384L$. At every length its output length $M$ satisfies
  \[
    L^{a+1}\le M\le2^a(L+1)^{a+1}.
  \]
  This is the factor-four entropy schedule above, not the sharper
  near-linear entropy dependence in Chattopadhyay--Goodman--Liao,
  Theorem 5.6. The quantifiers here fix both $a$ and $e$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply the finite schedule and seed bounds once the raw depth fits $L$.
  Ceiling-logarithm estimates give the output bounds. Exponential growth
  dominates every fixed polynomial in the input logarithm, giving sublinear
  entropy and absorbing the depth-dependent seed terms into $16384L$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Runtime numerical states and total parameter initialization]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-state}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockStateStep,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.encodeScheduledBlockState,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockStateStepEval,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockInputLog,

  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-parameters}
  Store the current width and entropy in unary, with fixed error exponent
  $E$. A numerical step computes the actual rate-three output half-width
  and divides the entropy by four. For arbitrary requested depth $r$ and
  error exponent $e$, the total initializer selects
  $h=\min(r,\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil)$, $E=e+h+2$, the explicit leaf
  reserve, and the actual rate-one initial compressed width.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Uniform generation and bounded iteration of the numerical schedule]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-state}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.explicitCondenserHalfWidth_unaryFn,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockStateStep_iterate,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockStateStepEval_encode,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockStateStepEval_iterate,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockStateStepEval_mem_FP,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockState_mem_FP,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockDepth_pow_four_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockRuntimeInitialWidth_bounds,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockRuntime_reserve_budget,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockInitialEntropy_unaryFn,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockRuntimeInitialWidth_unaryFn,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockStateInit_mem_FP,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockStateInit_iterate_mem_FP}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-state,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-reserve}
  The exact paired-condenser half-width and initialized schedule parameters
  have uniform polynomial-time unary computations. The encoded numerical
  transition computes the prescribed widths and entropies through every
  scheduled level. Clipping gives $4^h\le4(n+1)^2$, so the initializer
  computes its initial entropy by a bounded-power operation without a
  circular capacity premise. Its selected width contains that entropy
  and is at most $64$ times it, and its reserve pays the global split budget.
  The initialized numerical loop is polynomial-time uniformly up to any
  requested level clipped to $h$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Compose the unary half-width and bounded arithmetic certificates.
  Division by four matches the entropy recurrence through depth $h$.
  The independent bound on $4^h$ initializes the entropy; the explicit
  reserve then bounds every numerical state in the iterated evaluator.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Canonical flat encoding of the retained seeds]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-codec}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockSeedPrefixBits,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.encodeScheduledBlockSeedPrefix,

  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-extractor}
  Encode the initial field seed, followed by the fresh level seeds in
  increasing order, followed by the final condenser seed and hash seed.
  A prefix through any level and the complete tuple are each equivalent
  to Boolean words of their exact summed field widths. These semantic
  equivalences require no field-enumeration instances.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Exact seed widths, append order, and inverse laws]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-codec}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockSeedPrefixBits_zero,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockSeedPrefixBits_succ,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.encodeScheduledBlockSeedPrefix_zero,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.encodeScheduledBlockSeedPrefix_succ,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.encodeScheduledBlockSeedPrefix_length,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.decode_encodeScheduledBlockSeedPrefix,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.encode_decodeScheduledBlockSeedPrefix,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockSeedBits_eq_prefix,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.encodeScheduledBlockSeeds_append,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.encodeScheduledBlockSeeds_length,

    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.encode_decodeScheduledBlockSeeds}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-codec}
  Extending a prefix appends exactly the next fresh field word. The full
  word is $(\text{prefix}\mathbin{+\!+}\text{condenser})
  \mathbin{+\!+}\text{hash}$ and has exactly the specified seed budget $d$.
  Decoding each encoding recovers its tuple; encoding a decoded word
  recovers every word of the prescribed exact length. Zero depth is included.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Induct over the prefix and use the binary-field codec's exact length and
  inverse laws. Taking and dropping at those widths recover each adjacent
  component, including all coefficient zeros.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The encoded block-payload loop]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-run}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.ScheduledBlockRunState,

  A runtime state contains the numerical dimensions, block count,
  concatenated block payload, and unused seed suffix. Initial compression
  consumes its first seed word. Each later step consumes one fresh seed
  prefix, applies the actual shared-seed condenser to every block, and
  keeps its two halves consecutively. The loop executes $h$ such steps.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Uniform bounded execution of the complete payload loop]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-run}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockRunStepEval_encode,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockRunStepEval_iterate,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.encodeScheduledBlockRun_length,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockRun_dimensions,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockRun_count,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockRun_iterate_bound,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockRunStepEval_mem_FP,

  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-run,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-state,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-parameters,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-block-program}
  The string transition computes the structured state transition and its
  every iterate. The endpoint has width $n_h$, entropy $Q$, and $2^h$
  blocks. For $Q>0$ and the global reserve budget, every intermediate
  encoding has length at most $18N+2E+|s|+18$, where $N$ is the initial
  compressed width and $s$ is the original seed word. The entire initial
  compression and payload loop is uniformly polynomial-time when the
  numerical operands, including initial entropy, have unary certificates.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The exact state codec transfers the one-step computation to iteration.
  Sum the proved widths of the numerical workspace, unary count, payload,
  and remaining seeds. The finite schedule bounds these throughout the
  run, permitting bounded polynomial-time iteration.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact semantic correctness of every runtime level]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-run-correctness}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockRunInitial_eq,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockRun_iterate_eq_recursiveBlockMap,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockRun_eq_recursiveBlockMap}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-codec,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-run,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-block-correctness}
  On a Boolean input of width $n$ and a canonical seed prefix through
  $i\le h$ followed by any trailing word, the runtime state after $i$
  steps has exactly the prescribed width, entropy, and block count.
  Its payload is precisely the serialized recursive block tuple, in
  coordinate order, and its remaining seeds are exactly that trailing word.
  At $i=h$ this is the complete semantic leaf tuple with entropy $Q$.
  No entropy-budget or field-enumeration premise is required.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Recombining the initial output halves recovers the complete initial
  condenser word. Induct using the prefix append law and the one-level
  runtime correctness theorem, keeping the trailing word arbitrary.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[One shared final extractor on the serialized leaves]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-one-shot-block-program}

  and hash seed words, and concatenate their $\ell$-bit outputs in order.
  The number of blocks is given in unary; short payloads use the existing
  total fixed-width slicing convention.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Uniform leaf evaluation and exact output serialization]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-one-shot-block-program}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.oneShotBlockExtractorBits_length,

  On concatenated Boolean tuples and canonical field seeds it is exactly
  the serialized tuple of decoded one-shot outputs, with $0,1\in\mathbf F_2$
  represented by Boolean bits. The program is uniformly polynomial-time
  in the unary numerical parameters, payload, two seed words, and count,
  including zero widths and zero blocks.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Fixed-width slicing recovers each supplied block. Apply the one-shot
  correctness and length theorems, and concatenate the certified outputs
  over the unary range. The shared-seed statistical application follows
  Chattopadhyay--Goodman--Liao, Lemma 5.5; this node proves the runtime identity.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The total scheduled extractor program]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-program}

  Run initial compression and every internal level, then consume the
  final condenser/hash seed pair and extract all leaves. The total wrapper
  derives the source length from its actual input word and initializes
  the clipped schedule. Its single paired interface accepts a source,
  a flat seed word, unary requested depth, and unary error exponent.
  All projections and component programs are total on malformed encodings.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[One uniform polynomial-time evaluator for the complete schedule]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-program}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockExtractorBits_length,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockExtractorBits_mem_FP,

  The specified bit program emits exactly $2^h\ell$ bits. Under its
  positive-reserve and global-budget premises it has a uniform
  polynomial-time certificate in all certified unary parameters and input
  words. The total paired evaluator generates parameters satisfying these
  premises and belongs to $\mathrm{FP}$ on every input word, without any
  externally supplied numerical validity certificate. A requested depth
  already at most $\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil$ is left unchanged.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Compose the total parameter initializer, bounded payload iteration, and
  final shared-seed leaf program. The initializer supplies the reserve
  premises internally, and all numerical values have unary certificates.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The complete bit program computes the statistical extractor]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-program-correctness}

  For every natural parameter choice, Boolean source, and complete semantic
  seed tuple, the bit program at its canonical seed encoding returns
  exactly the flattened scheduled extractor output, with each
  $\mathbf F_2$ coordinate encoded by its Boolean equality test with $1$.
  This identity has no reserve, entropy, or field-enumeration premise.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Exact loop correctness leaves the semantic leaf payload and the final
  condenser/hash seed words. The seed widths split that suffix exactly,
  and the leaf-program identity returns precisely the scheduled output.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The actual extractor with Boolean inputs, seeds, and outputs]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-boolean}

  blocks of $\ell$ Boolean coordinates. This definition directly runs the
  bit program and invokes no abstract field enumeration.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The actual Boolean program is a linear strong extractor]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-boolean}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockBooleanExtractor_eq,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockBooleanExtractor_xor,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockBooleanExtractor_weighted,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockBooleanExtractor_dyadic}
  \leanok
